   220→async function runCase(testCase: GoldenCase): Promise<ExtractionEvalOutput> {
   221→  const cacheKey = buildCaseCacheKey(extractionModel, testCase);
   222→  const cached = resultCache[cacheKey];
   223→  if (cached) {
   224→    return {
   225→      ...cached,
   226→      extractedTools: cached.extractedTools ?? [],
   227→      extractedRelations: cached.extractedRelations ?? [],
   228→    };
   229→  }
   230→
   231→  const { workspaceRecord, workspaceName, projectRecord, conversationRecord, ownerPersonCount } = await seedWorkspace(runtime.surreal, testCase.workspace_name);
   232→  const conversationId = conversationRecord.id as string;
   233→  const seededContext = testCase.context ?? [];
   234→  const contextMessageIds = seededContext.length > 0
   235→    ? await seedConversationContext(runtime.surreal, conversationRecord, seededContext)
   236→    : [];
   237→
   238→  if (testCase.workspace_seed && testCase.workspace_seed.length > 0) {
   239→    await seedGraphEntities(runtime.surreal, workspaceRecord, projectRecord, conversationRecord, testCase.workspace_seed);
   240→  }
   241→
   242→  const userMessageRecord = await seedUserMessage(runtime.surreal, conversationRecord, testCase.input);
   243→
   244→  const extractionConversationContext = await loadExtractionConversationContext({
   245→    surreal: runtime.surreal,
   246→    conversationId,
   247→    currentMessageRecord: userMessageRecord,
   248→  });
   249→  const extractionGraphContext = await loadConversationGraphContext(runtime.surreal, conversationId, 60);
   250→  const workspaceProjects = await loadWorkspaceProjects(runtime.surreal, workspaceRecord);
   251→  const workspaceProjectNames = workspaceProjects.map((project) => project.name);
   252→
   253→  const extraction = await extractStructuredGraph({
   254→    extractionModel: runtime.extractionModel,
   255→    conversationHistory: extractionConversationContext.conversationHistory,
   256→    currentMessage: extractionConversationContext.currentMessage,
   257→    graphContext: extractionGraphContext,
   258→    sourceText: testCase.input,
   259→    onboarding: true,
   260→    workspaceName,
   261→    projectNames: workspaceProjectNames,
   262→  });
   263→
   264→  const now = new Date();
   265→  const persistence = await persistExtractionOutput({
   266→    surreal: runtime.surreal,
   267→    embeddingModel: runtime.embeddingModel,
   268→    embeddingDimension: runtime.config.embeddingDimension,
   269→    extractionModelId: runtime.config.extractionModelId,
   270→    extractionStoreThreshold: runtime.config.extractionStoreThreshold,
   271→    workspaceRecord,
   272→    sourceRecord: userMessageRecord as SourceRecord,
   273→    sourceKind: "message",
   274→    sourceLabel: testCase.input.slice(0, 140),
   275→    promptText: testCase.input,
   276→    output: extraction,
   277→    sourceMessageRecord: userMessageRecord,
   278→    extractionHistoryMessageIds: extractionConversationContext.conversationHistory.map(
   279→      (row) => row.id.id as string,
   280→    ),
   281→    now,
   282→  });
   283→
   284→  await appendExtractedTools(runtime.surreal, workspaceRecord, persistence.tools, now);
   285→
   286→  const [evidenceRows] = await runtime.surreal
   287→    .query<[Array<{
   288→      evidence?: string;
   289→      from_text?: string;
   290→      model?: string;
   291→      evidence_source?: RecordId<"message", string>;
   292→      resolved_from?: RecordId<"message", string>;
   293→    }>]>(
   294→      "SELECT evidence, from_text, model, evidence_source, resolved_from FROM extraction_relation WHERE `in` = $source;",
   295→      { source: userMessageRecord },
   296→    )
   297→    .collect<[Array<{
   298→      evidence?: string;
   299→      from_text?: string;
   300→      model?: string;
   301→      evidence_source?: RecordId<"message", string>;
   302→      resolved_from?: RecordId<"message", string>;
   303→    }>]>();
   304→
   305→  const personCount = await loadWorkspacePeopleCount(runtime.surreal, workspaceRecord);
   306→  const [workspaceToolRows] = await runtime.surreal
   307→    .query<[Array<{ tools?: string[] }>]>("SELECT tools FROM $workspace LIMIT 1;", {
   308→      workspace: workspaceRecord,
   309→    })
   310→    .collect<[Array<{ tools?: string[] }>]>();
   311→  const extractedTools = workspaceToolRows[0]?.tools ?? [];
   312→
   313→  const [relationRows] = await runtime.surreal
   314→    .query<[Array<{
   315→      kind: string;
   316→      in: { tb: string };
   317→      out: { tb: string };
   318→      from_text: string;
   319→      to_text: string;
   320→      confidence: number;
   321→    }>]>(
   322→      "SELECT kind, `in`, out, from_text, to_text, confidence FROM entity_relation WHERE source_message = $source AND kind != 'POSSIBLE_DUPLICATE';",
   323→      { source: userMessageRecord },
   324→    )
   325→    .collect<[Array<{
   326→      kind: string;
   327→      in: { tb: string };
   328→      out: { tb: string };
   329→      from_text: string;
   330→      to_text: string;
   331→      confidence: number;
   332→    }>]>();
   333→
   334→  const output: ExtractionEvalOutput = {
   335→    caseId: testCase.id,
   336→    input: testCase.input,
   337→    userMessageId: userMessageRecord.id as string,
   338→    contextMessageIds,
   339→    extractedEntities: persistence.entities.map((e) => ({
   340→      kind: e.kind,
   341→      text: e.text,
   342→      confidence: e.confidence,
   343→      ...(e.category ? { category: e.category } : {}),
   344→    })),
   345→    extractedTools,
   346→    personCount,
   347→    ownerPersonCount,
   348→    evidenceRows: evidenceRows.map((row) => ({
   349→      evidence: row.evidence,
   350→      fromText: row.from_text,
   351→      model: row.model,
   352→      evidenceSourceId: row.evidence_source?.id as string | undefined,
   353→      resolvedFromId: row.resolved_from?.id as string | undefined,
   354→    })),
   355→    extractedRelations: relationRows.map((row) => ({
   356→      kind: row.kind,
   357→      fromKind: row.in.tb,
   358→      fromText: row.from_text,
   359→      toKind: row.out.tb,
   360→      toText: row.to_text,
   361→      confidence: row.confidence,
   362→    })),
   363→  };
   364→
   365→  resultCache[cacheKey] = output;
   366→  saveCache(cachePath, resultCache);
   367→  return output;
   368→}
   369→
   370→function loadCache(path: string): Record<string, ExtractionEvalOutput> {
   371→  if (!existsSync(path)) {
   372→    return {};
   373→  }
   374→
   375→  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, "utf8")) as Record<string, ExtractionEvalOutput>;
   376→}
   377→
   378→function saveCache(path: string, cache: Record<string, ExtractionEvalOutput>): void {
   379→  mkdirSync(cacheDir, { recursive: true });
   380→  writeFileSync(path, JSON.stringify(cache, null, 2));
   381→}
   382→
   383→function assertAutoevalEnv(): void {
   384→  const hasOpenAiKey = hasEnv("OPENAI_API_KEY");
   385→  const hasBraintrustKey = hasEnv("BRAINTRUST_API_KEY");
   386→  if (!hasOpenAiKey && !hasBraintrustKey) {
   387→    throw new Error(
   388→      "Missing evaluator credentials. Set OPENAI_API_KEY (recommended for OPENAI_BASE_URL/OpenRouter) or BRAINTRUST_API_KEY.",
   389→    );
   390→  }
   391→
   392→  if (!hasEnv("OPENAI_BASE_URL")) {
   393→    throw new Error("Missing OPENAI_BASE_URL for autoevals provider routing.");
   394→  }
   395→}
   396→
   397→function requireEnv(name: string): string {
   398→  const value = process.env[name];
   399→  if (!value || value.trim().length === 0) {
   400→    throw new Error(`Missing required environment variable: ${name}`);
   401→  }
   402→  return value;
   403→}
   404→
   405→function hasEnv(name: string): boolean {
   406→  const value = process.env[name];
   407→  return value !== undefined && value.trim().length > 0;
   408→}
   409→
   410→function buildCaseCacheKey(modelId: string, testCase: GoldenCase): string {
   411→  const cacheVersion = "classification-v7";
   412→  const caseHash = createHash("sha256").update(JSON.stringify(testCase)).digest("hex").slice(0, 24);
   413→  return `${cacheVersion}:${modelId}:${testCase.id}:${caseHash}`;
   414→}

    54→    if (input.sourceKind === "message" && !input.sourceMessageRecord) {
    55→      throw new Error("message extraction persistence requires sourceMessageRecord");
    56→    }
    57→
    58→    const extractionHistoryMessageIds = new Set(input.extractionHistoryMessageIds ?? []);
    59→    const relationships = postValidateRelationships({
    60→      relationships: input.output.relationships,
    61→      storeThreshold: input.extractionStoreThreshold,
    62→    }).map((relationship) => ({
    63→      ...relationship,
    64→      kind: normalizeRelationshipKind(relationship.kind),
    65→    }));
    66→
    67→    const persistedEntities: ExtractedEntity[] = [];
    68→    const persistedRelationships: ExtractedRelationship[] = [];

   222→async function applyAssigneeReference(input: {
   223→  surreal: Surreal;
   224→  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;

   234→
   235→  const personRecord = await findWorkspacePersonByName({
   236→    surreal: input.surreal,
   237→    workspaceRecord: input.workspaceRecord,
   238→    personName: assigneeName,
   239→  });
   240→
   241→  const patch = resolvePersonAttributionPatch({
   242→    targetKind: input.entityKind,
   243→    assigneeName,
   244→    ...(personRecord ? { personRecordId: personRecord.id as string } : {}),
   245→  });
   246→
   247→  if (patch.kind === "feature") {
   248→    if (patch.field === "owner" && personRecord) {
   249→      await input.surreal.update(input.entityRecord as RecordId<"feature", string>).merge({
   250→        owner: personRecord,
   251→        updated_at: input.now,
   252→      });
   253→      return { resolved: true, assigneeName };
   254→    }
   255→
   256→    await input.surreal.update(input.entityRecord as RecordId<"feature", string>).merge({
   257→      owner_name: patch.value,
   258→      updated_at: input.now,
   259→    });
   260→    return { resolved: false, assigneeName };
   261→  }
   262→
   263→  if (patch.kind === "task") {
   264→    if (patch.field === "owner" && personRecord) {
   265→      await input.surreal.update(input.entityRecord as RecordId<"task", string>).merge({
   266→        owner: personRecord,
   267→        updated_at: input.now,
   268→      });
   269→      return { resolved: true, assigneeName };
   270→    }
   271→
   272→    await input.surreal.update(input.entityRecord as RecordId<"task", string>).merge({
   273→      owner_name: patch.value,
   274→      updated_at: input.now,
   275→    });
   276→    return { resolved: false, assigneeName };
   277→  }
   278→
   279→  if (patch.kind === "decision") {
   280→    if (patch.field === "decided_by" && personRecord) {
   281→      await input.surreal.update(input.entityRecord as RecordId<"decision", string>).merge({
   282→        decided_by: personRecord,
   283→        updated_at: input.now,
   284→      });
   285→      return { resolved: true, assigneeName };
   286→    }
   287→
   288→    await input.surreal.update(input.entityRecord as RecordId<"decision", string>).merge({
   289→      decided_by_name: patch.value,
   290→      updated_at: input.now,
   291→    });
   292→    return { resolved: false, assigneeName };
   293→  }
   294→
   295→  if (patch.field === "assigned_to" && personRecord) {
   296→    await input.surreal.update(input.entityRecord as RecordId<"question", string>).merge({
   297→      assigned_to: personRecord,
   298→      updated_at: input.now,
   299→    });
   300→    return { resolved: true, assigneeName };
   301→  }
   302→
   303→  await input.surreal.update(input.entityRecord as RecordId<"question", string>).merge({
   304→    assigned_to_name: patch.value,
   305→    updated_at: input.now,
   306→  });
   307→  return { resolved: false, assigneeName };
   308→}
   309→
   310→export async function appendExtractedTools(
   311→  surreal: Surreal,
   312→  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>,
   313→  extractedTools: string[],
   314→  now: Date,
   315→): Promise<void> {
   316→  const dedupedTools = [...new Set(extractedTools.map((tool) => tool.trim()).filter((tool) => tool.length > 0))];
   317→  if (extractedTools.length > 0 && dedupedTools.length > 0) {
   318→    await appendWorkspaceTools(surreal, workspaceRecord, dedupedTools, now);
   319→  }
   320→}
   321→

> AGENT

Now I have a clear picture. Two fixes needed:

1. **`resolvedFromMessageId` crash** — The LLM hallucinating a self-referencing or non-existent message ID is external input validation, not an internal contract violation. Change the throws to warning logs + return `undefined`.

2. **Deterministic eval IDs** — Replace `randomUUID()` in eval-test-kit with deterministic hashes seeded from test case ID, so identical test cases produce identical prompts across runs.

Let me check what logging utilities are available, then implement both fixes.

{

  "old_string": "import type { SourceKind } from \"../../shared/contracts\";\n\nexport function resolveValidatedResolvedFromMessageId(input: {\n  resolvedFromMessageId?: string;\n  sourceKind: SourceKind;\n  sourceMessageId?: string;\n  extractionHistoryMessageIds: Set<string>;\n}): string | undefined {\n  const resolvedFromMessageId = input.resolvedFromMessageId?.trim();\n  if (!resolvedFromMessageId) {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  if (input.sourceKind !== \"message\") {\n    throw new Error(\"resolvedFromMessageId is only valid for message extraction sources\");\n  }\n\n  if (!input.sourceMessageId || input.sourceMessageId.trim().length === 0) {\n    throw new Error(\"resolvedFromMessageId requires sourceMessageId\");\n  }\n\n  if (resolvedFromMessageId === input.sourceMessageId) {\n    throw new Error(\"resolvedFromMessageId cannot match the current source message id\");\n  }\n\n  if (!input.extractionHistoryMessageIds.has(resolvedFromMessageId)) {\n    throw new Error(`resolvedFromMessageId is not present in extraction conversation history: ${resolvedFromMessageId}`);\n  }\n\n  return resolvedFromMessageId;\n}",
  "new_string": "import type { SourceKind } from \"../../shared/contracts\";\nimport { logWarn } from \"../http/observability\";\n\nexport function resolveValidatedResolvedFromMessageId(input: {\n  resolvedFromMessageId?: string;\n  sourceKind: SourceKind;\n  sourceMessageId?: string;\n  extractionHistoryMessageIds: Set<string>;\n}): string | undefined {\n  const resolvedFromMessageId = input.resolvedFromMessageId?.trim();\n  if (!resolvedFromMessageId) {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  if (input.sourceKind !== \"message\") {\n    throw new Error(\"resolvedFromMessageId is only valid for message extraction sources\");\n  }\n\n  if (!input.sourceMessageId || input.sourceMessageId.trim().length === 0) {\n    throw new Error(\"resolvedFromMessageId requires sourceMessageId\");\n  }\n\n  if (resolvedFromMessageId === input.sourceMessageId) {\n    logWarn(\"extraction.provenance.self_reference\", \"LLM returned resolvedFromMessageId matching current source message; discarding\", {\n      resolvedFromMessageId,\n      sourceMessageId: input.sourceMessageId,\n    });\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  if (!input.extractionHistoryMessageIds.has(resolvedFromMessageId)) {\n    logWarn(\"extraction.provenance.unknown_message\", \"LLM returned resolvedFromMessageId not in conversation history; discarding\", {\n      resolvedFromMessageId,\n    });\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  return resolvedFromMessageId;\n}"
}

{

  "file_path": "/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/palembang/evals/eval-test-kit.ts",
  "old_string": "import { readFileSync } from \"node:fs\";\nimport { join } from \"node:path\";\nimport { randomUUID } from \"node:crypto\";\nimport { createOpenRouter } from \"@openrouter/ai-sdk-provider\";\nimport { RecordId, Surreal } from \"surrealdb\";\nimport type { StreamEvent } from \"../app/src/shared/contracts\";\nimport type { SseRegistry } from \"../app/src/server/streaming/sse-registry\";\nimport type { ServerConfig } from \"../app/src/server/runtime/config\";\nimport type { WorkspaceSeedItem } from \"./types\";",
  "new_string": "import { readFileSync } from \"node:fs\";\nimport { join } from \"node:path\";\nimport { randomUUID, createHash } from \"node:crypto\";\nimport { createOpenRouter } from \"@openrouter/ai-sdk-provider\";\nimport { RecordId, Surreal } from \"surrealdb\";\nimport type { StreamEvent } from \"../app/src/shared/contracts\";\nimport type { SseRegistry } from \"../app/src/server/streaming/sse-registry\";\nimport type { ServerConfig } from \"../app/src/server/runtime/config\";\nimport type { WorkspaceSeedItem } from \"./types\";\n\nexport function createDeterministicIdGenerator(seed: string): () => string {\n  let counter = 0;\n  return () => {\n    const hash = createHash(\"sha256\").update(`${seed}:${counter++}`).digest(\"hex\");\n    return `${hash.slice(0, 8)}-${hash.slice(8, 12)}-${hash.slice(12, 16)}-${hash.slice(16, 20)}-${hash.slice(20, 32)}`;\n  };\n}"
}

Here's a summary of what was fixed:

**1. `resolvedFromMessageId` crash fix** (`provenance.ts`)
- Changed the self-referencing and not-in-history checks from hard `throw` to `logWarn` + `return undefined`
- These are LLM hallucination cases (external input), not internal contract violations — graceful degradation is correct here
- Internal contract violations (wrong `sourceKind`, missing `sourceMessageId`) still throw

**2. Deterministic eval IDs** (`eval-test-kit.ts` + `extraction.eval.ts`)
- Added `createDeterministicIdGenerator(seed)` that produces UUID-formatted hashes from a counter
- All seed functions (`seedWorkspace`, `seedConversationContext`, `seedUserMessage`, `seedGraphEntities`) now accept an optional `nextId` parameter
- `runCase` creates a deterministic generator seeded with `testCase.id`, so the same test case always produces identical record IDs across runs
- This eliminates prompt variance from `randomUUID()`, making `temperature: 0` effective

**3. Cache version bumped** to `classification-v8` since deterministic IDs change the prompts.

All 125 unit tests pass. You can run `bun run eval` to verify the eval suite no longer crashes and produces consistent results across runs.

# Prompt Caching Overview

## How It Works
Prompt caching automatically routes API requests to servers that recently processed identical prompt prefixes. The system hashes the initial prompt portion (typically the first 256 tokens) to determine routing. If a matching prefix exists in cache, the system retrieves it; otherwise, it processes the full prompt and caches the prefix for future requests.

## Cost & Latency Benefits
- **Latency reduction:** Up to 80% decrease
- **Input token cost reduction:** Up to 90% savings
- **Additional fees:** None—caching is automatic with no extra charges

## Minimum Requirements
- Prompts must contain **1,024 tokens or longer** to enable caching
- Requests under 1,024 tokens display `cached_tokens: 0` in usage metrics
- All recent models (gpt-4o and newer) support caching

## What Can Be Cached
- Complete messages arrays (system, user, assistant)
- Images (links or base64-encoded)
- Tool definitions
- Structured output schemas

## Cache Duration Policies

**In-memory retention (default):**
- Active for 5–10 minutes of inactivity
- Maximum 1-hour retention
- Available for all compatible models

**Extended retention (24-hour):**
- Available for gpt-5.2, gpt-5.1, gpt-5, gpt-4.1, and related variants
- Persists key/value tensors to GPU-local storage
- Maximum 24-hour retention

## Configuration
Set `prompt_cache_retention` parameter: `"in_memory"` or `"24h"`

## Key Optimization Strategy
Place "static or repeated content at the beginning" and dynamic content at the end. Use the optional `prompt_cache_key` parameter to influence routing, keeping each unique prefix-key combination below ~15 requests per minute to avoid overflow.

## Structured Outputs Compatibility
Yes—structured output schemas are cached as part of the system message prefix.

## Research Complete: GPT-5 Mini vs o4-mini for Structured Extraction

**Output file**: `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/palembang/docs/research/model-evaluation/gpt5-mini-vs-o4-mini-extraction.md`

### Top-Line Findings

**GPT-5 mini is the recommended candidate. o4-mini is not recommended.**

### Pricing Comparison

| Model | Input $/M | Output $/M | Cached $/M | TTFT | Speed |
|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------|-------|
| **gpt-4.1-mini** (current) | $0.40 | $1.60 | $0.10 | ~0.49s | ~64 tok/s |
| **GPT-5 mini** (minimal) | $0.25 | $2.00 | $0.025 | ~0.78s | ~77 tok/s |
| **o4-mini** | $1.10 | $4.40+ | $0.275 | ~58s | ~115 tok/s |

### Critical Migration Issue: `temperature: 0` is not supported

The current extraction code at `app/src/server/extraction/extract-graph.ts:35` explicitly sets `temperature: 0`. **Neither GPT-5 mini nor o4-mini supports this parameter** -- both reasoning models fix temperature at 1 and reject any other value. This will cause an API error if not addressed before switching models.

The alternative for GPT-5 mini is `reasoning_effort: "minimal"`, which OpenAI explicitly recommends for "deterministic, lightweight tasks (extraction, formatting, short rewrites, simple classification)." At minimal effort, GPT-5 mini produces few or no reasoning tokens, keeping output fast (~0.78s TTFT) and cost-predictable.

### Why GPT-5 mini over o4-mini

1. **Cost**: 4.4x cheaper input, 2.2x cheaper output than o4-mini
2. **Latency**: ~0.78s TTFT at minimal effort vs ~58s for o4-mini (unacceptable for real-time chat)
3. **Context**: 400K tokens vs 200K (both sufficient, GPT-5 mini has more headroom)
4. **Purpose-fit**: OpenAI recommends minimal-effort GPT-5 mini for extraction tasks
5. **Succession**: GPT-5 mini is the official successor to o4-mini (Aug 2025 vs Apr 2025)

### Why gpt-4.1-mini may still be the best option

The current model has advantages worth considering:
- **Lowest TTFT**: ~0.49s vs ~0.78s for GPT-5 mini minimal
- **Temperature 0 support**: Works today, no code changes needed
- **Cheapest output tokens**: $1.60/M vs $2.00/M
- **Largest context window**: 1M tokens vs 400K
- **No reasoning overhead**: Zero risk of unexpected reasoning token billing

GPT-5 mini wins on input pricing ($0.25 vs $0.40/M) and cached input pricing ($0.025 vs $0.10/M). For input-heavy extraction, total cost is slightly lower with GPT-5 mini.

### Knowledge Gaps

- No direct extraction quality benchmarks between these models -- **run the eval suite** to measure
- Unverified whether OpenRouter correctly passes `reasoning_effort: "minimal"` to GPT-5 mini
- No data on comparative structured output error rates through OpenRouter

### Recommended Next Steps

1. Run `bun run eval` with `EXTRACTION_MODEL=openai/gpt-5-mini` and compare against the gpt-4.1-mini baseline
2. Measure actual reasoning token counts and TTFT through OpenRouter with minimal effort
3. If switching, update `extract-graph.ts` to conditionally skip `temperature: 0` for reasoning models

Good research. Here's the breakdown:

## Prompt Caching (OpenAI)

Caching is **automatic** and free — no code changes needed. Key details:

- **90% discount** on cached input tokens, up to **80% latency reduction**
- Minimum **1,024 tokens** to activate (your extraction prompts easily exceed this)
- **Structured output schemas are cached** as part of the system message prefix
- Cache lives 5-10 min (default) or **24h** for gpt-4.1 and gpt-5 family (`prompt_cache_retention: "24h"`)
- Optimization: put static content (system prompt, schema) first, dynamic content (user message) last — which is already how your extraction prompt is structured

You're likely already benefiting from this during eval runs since the system prompt is identical across all 45 cases. You can confirm by checking for `cached_tokens` in the API response usage.

## GPT-5 mini vs o4-mini vs gpt-4.1-mini (current)

| | gpt-4.1-mini (current) | GPT-5 mini | o4-mini |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Input $/M** | $0.40 | $0.25 | $1.10 |
| **Output $/M** | $1.60 | $2.00 | $4.40+ |
| **Cached input $/M** | $0.10 | $0.025 | $0.275 |
| **TTFT** | ~0.49s | ~0.78s | **~58s** |
| **Context** | 1M | 400K | 200K |
| **temperature: 0** | Yes | **No** (reasoning model) | **No** |

**o4-mini is out** — 58s TTFT is unacceptable for real-time chat extraction, and it's 4x more expensive on input.

**GPT-5 mini is interesting** mainly for caching economics ($0.025/M cached vs $0.10/M). But there's a breaking change: it's a reasoning model and **doesn't support `temperature: 0`**. You'd need `reasoning_effort: "minimal"` instead, which OpenAI recommends specifically for extraction/classification tasks. Unclear if OpenRouter passes that parameter correctly.

**gpt-4.1-mini is probably still the best fit** today:
- Fastest TTFT (0.49s)
- `temperature: 0` works (deterministic, no code changes)
- Cheapest output tokens ($1.60/M)
- Largest context (1M)
- Known behavior in your eval suite

### If you want to try GPT-5 mini

You'd need to:
1. Conditionally skip `temperature: 0` for reasoning models in `extract-graph.ts`
2. Pass `reasoning_effort: "minimal"` via provider options
3. Run the eval suite with `EXTRACTION_MODEL=openai/gpt-5-mini` and compare scores

Worth testing if the cached input savings matter at your volume, but I'd establish the deterministic baseline with gpt-4.1-mini first (the changes we just made), then experiment with GPT-5 mini as a follow-up.

> DEVELOPER

oh gpt-5 mini has a reasoning model but gpt-4.1-mini does not?

> AGENT

Correct. OpenAI's model families split differently:

- **gpt-4.1 family** (gpt-4.1, gpt-4.1-mini, gpt-4.1-nano) — traditional completion models. Support `temperature: 0`, no reasoning tokens, straightforward structured output.

- **gpt-5 family** (gpt-5, gpt-5-mini, gpt-5-audio) — reasoning models (like o-series). Fixed `temperature: 1`, use `reasoning_effort` parameter instead, emit internal reasoning tokens that you get billed for.

So gpt-4.1-mini and gpt-5-mini are **not** direct successors in the way you'd expect from the naming. gpt-5-mini is more like the successor to **o4-mini** (reasoning), while gpt-4.1-mini doesn't have a direct gpt-5-era non-reasoning replacement yet.

For your extraction use case — structured JSON output with deterministic results — gpt-4.1-mini is the right model class. Reasoning is wasted overhead for "parse this text and fill in a schema."

Based on the search results, here's a comprehensive comparison of GPT-4.1 Mini and Claude 3.5 Haiku (also referred to as Claude Haiku 4.5 in newer documentation) regarding structured output extraction:

## Structured Output Capabilities

GPT-4.1 Mini by OpenAI can generate structured data, and it supports structured outputs for predictable schemas and function calling for building tool-augmented agents.

For Claude models, the search results mention capabilities for structured output handling, though the newer Claude Haiku 4.5 version information is more prominent in recent 2025-2026 comparisons.

## Key Performance Differences

**GPT-4.1 Mini:**
- Released by OpenAI on April 14, 2025, it matches or exceeds GPT-4o in many benchmarks while reducing latency by nearly half and cost by 83%
- It features a 1 million token context window and can generate up to 32K tokens per request, with a knowledge cutoff of June 2024 and costs $0.40 per million tokens for input and $1.60 per million tokens for output

**Claude Haiku 4.5:**
- Released October 15, 2025, it is Anthropic's ultra-fast, cost-efficient small model
- It has more recent training data (2025-02-28 vs June 2024)
- Claude Haiku 4.5 is roughly 3.0x more expensive compared to GPT-4.1 Mini for input and output tokens

## Use Case Suitability

GPT-4.1 Mini excels at classification, tagging, entity extraction, rewriting, paraphrasing, and SEO tasks, making it particularly strong for structured data extraction workflows.

> AGENT

Here's the head-to-head for your extraction use case:

| | gpt-4.1-mini | Claude 3.5 Haiku |
|---|---|---|
| **Input $/M** | $0.40 | $1.00 |
| **Output $/M** | $1.60 | $5.00 |
| **Cached input $/M** | $0.10 | $0.10 (Anthropic) |
| **Context window** | 1M | 200K |
| **temperature: 0** | Yes | Yes |
| **Structured output** | Native JSON schema | Tool use workaround |
| **Speed** | ~64 tok/s | ~78 tok/s |

**gpt-4.1-mini wins on cost by a wide margin** — 2.5x cheaper on input, 3x cheaper on output. For extraction where the system prompt + schema is large and repeated, that adds up fast.

**Structured output is the big differentiator.** gpt-4.1-mini has native structured output support — you pass a JSON schema and the model is constrained to only produce valid JSON matching it. Claude 3.5 Haiku doesn't have native structured output; the Vercel AI SDK uses tool-call mode as a workaround, which is less reliable and can produce schema violations.

Your codebase default (`anthropic/claude-3.5-haiku` in `extraction.eval.ts:39`) is the fallback, but you've been running with `openai/gpt-4.1-mini` via env var. That's the right call — gpt-4.1-mini is cheaper, has better structured output compliance, and has a much larger context window for the extraction prompt + conversation history.

The one scenario where Haiku might edge ahead is raw classification quality on ambiguous cases, but you'd need to run the eval suite with both to know. Given the 3x price difference and native schema support, gpt-4.1-mini is the stronger default for this workload.

Sources:
- [Claude 3.5 Haiku on OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai/anthropic/claude-3.5-haiku)
- [GPT-4.1 Mini vs Claude Haiku comparison](https://blog.galaxy.ai/compare/claude-haiku-4-5-vs-gpt-4-1-mini)
- [Claude 3.5 Haiku pricing](https://cloudprice.net/models/openrouter/anthropic/claude-3-5-haiku)
